Any surprising result now ships with three numbers first: **(1) did the input VARY** (distinct-value count — one effective value cannot move anything, which is the ceiling defect exactly) · **(2) did it ARRIVE** (consuming file/line + rows surviving the join; a silent inner join is how a real signal becomes a clean null) · **(3) could the test have FIRED** (known-positive control). Missing any one + surprising = **suspected defect, not finding.** State what you expected BEFORE looking. **The tell is size, not direction:** an oracle that buys almost nothing is more suspicious than a modest honest effect. Register row 18 reopens the Vegas +0.23 on exactly this. **FOR A SPECIFICALLY:** three register rows moved off you onto D (13 snap_counts, 14 routes, 15 advanced_stats/props) plus new 16/17/18 — per Rule 3b those were stewardship legwork mis-routed to the gatekeeper. - [ ] 2026-08-16 · pace-of-play study (A-lane) · 🏁 **PACE IS A PUBLISHED NULL — NOTHING SHIPS, NO DECISION NEEDED, NO DIFF PREPARED.** Cory asked whether we had studied pace. We had not: `own_model_v5.py`'s `pace_lam` is regression-to-the-mean on prior-season team target/rush VOLUME, inert at QB and RB and a no-op at WR (λ=1.00); `own_model_v6.py` contains the string zero times. Now measured from real nflverse play-by-play. **The shape of the finding, which is more useful than "null":** what everybody MEANS by pace — plays per game — **does not carry year to year** (+0.062 [−0.154, +0.265]); what does carry is **tempo, seconds per play** (+0.417 [+0.191, +0.602], more persistent than the week-1 implied team total at +0.302); tempo **is** orthogonal to implied total (−0.140 [−0.358, +0.077]), so the premise held; and it **still** does not pay because `tempo(Y−1) → plays(Y)` cannot be resolved from zero (−0.181 [−0.368, +0.014]) — measured without reading one fantasy point. Graded anyway on `own_v6`'s own cells: bar not cleared, and the **negative control beats `own_v6` at WR on both metrics** while the registered sign does not, which voids the arm there. **And it is not a "too small to see" null** — the tilt moves CeeDee Lamb +7.1, Rachaad White −10.0 and reorders 49/60 WRs in the draftable range while buying nothing. Verdict `draft/audit/pace_of_play_2026-08-16.md`; prereg + two amendments committed before each step they govern. **Kept for later:** `team_pace_2021_2025.json` (team-season AND team-week, 32/32 measured every season) is there if a weekly arm or in-season usage tool ever wants tempo. **Nothing in `own_model_v*`, `build.py`, `own_projections.py`, `vorp.py`, `projections.py`, `fetch_component_stats.py` or any workflow was touched.**

- [ ] 2026-08-16 · C · 🧾 **TWO OF YOUR STORES DISAGREE ABOUT 2025, AND THE CAUSE IS VISIBLE IN THEIR OWN PROVENANCE. Not urgent, not touched, both files are yours.** Found while building `draft/backtest/tiered_outcome_model.py`, which re-scores `component_stats_*` as a data-integrity gate.
  **THE CHECK.** Score every `component_stats_{season}` player-week with `draft.scoring.score_stat_line` under the weekly-points store's own scoring table (fingerprint `bd8f3e50bd67a9ce`) and compare, row by row, against `nflverse_weekly_points_{season}`.
  **2023 and 2024 match EXACTLY — 5,055 and 4,984 shared player-weeks over weeks 1-17, max absolute difference 0.00 on both.** That is a strong statement about both stores: two independently built artifacts agreeing to the cent.
  **🔴 2025 DOES NOT.** `component_stats_2025.json` carries **1,067 player-weeks the points store does not have**, and of the 4,685 shared rows **120 differ — 101 of them by exactly −2.0**, the points store scoring a `fum_lost` the component store dropped (max difference 4.0).
  **THE CAUSE IS IN `provenance.url`: 2021-2024 came from `player_stats_{year}.parquet`; 2025 came from `stats_player_week_2025.parquet`.** Different nflverse release, different row population, and `fum_lost` is evidently not carried the same way. `advanced_stats_*` uses the `stats_player_week` schema throughout by design and says so; `component_stats` switched families at 2025 without the change being visible anywhere but the URL.
  **WHY IT MATTERS AND WHY IT IS NOT AN EMERGENCY.** Nothing on the board reads component stats for realized points — the points store is the outcome authority and is unaffected. But **v5/v6's component arms are FITTED on `component_stats`**, so for 2025 they are fitted on a slightly different season than the one they are graded against, and any future work that re-derives points from components (see the DECISIONS-NEEDED item I filed today about building 2021/2022 weekly stores) is proven only for the `player_stats_*` family.
  **NOT TOUCHED — both files carry `# TERRITORY: A`.** Reproduce with `TIERED_STORE_DIR=<dir> python -c "import tiered_outcome_model as T; print(T.verify_derivation((2023,2024,2025)))"`. My recommendation: record the release switch in `component_stats_2025.json`'s `_note` at minimum, and decide whether the 2025 store should be refetched from `player_stats_2025.parquet` for family consistency — that is a judgement about which release is more correct, and I have no basis to make it.

- [ ] 2026-08-16 · C · 🕰️ **`own_model_v2.board_ages()` LEAKS THE FUTURE, MILDLY, AND IT WILL LEAK MORE THE FURTHER BACK ANY BACKTEST REACHES.** Recorded rather than acted on; it is your file and the current exposure is small.
  It reads `age` from `public/draft_data.json` — the **2026** board — and hands it to fits graded on earlier seasons. The value is one year stale per season, which is fixable arithmetic. **The part that is not arithmetic is MEMBERSHIP: a player who retired after the graded season is simply absent from the 2026 board, so "has an age at all" is information from after the season being graded.** With v6 graded on 2025 that is one year of survivorship and the effect is small. It is the reason the tiered outcome model **excludes age entirely** rather than using it with a missing-indicator — at a 2023 test season the missingness pattern is a straightforward leak, and an indicator would have made it a feature.
  **Nothing to do before the 22nd.** Flagging it so that if the projection programme gains 2023/2024 as graded seasons (the DECISIONS-NEEDED item filed today would allow exactly that), the age term does not inherit the leak silently.

  **THE MECHANISM: the board is REBUILT at 23:13 and then EDITED IN PLACE by later commits.** `built_at` is written by the build, so after the first in-place edit it stops describing the artifact and starts describing an event in its past.   **✅ RESOLVED 2026-08-15 (relay session, working A's queue):** both instances fixed to content-addressing, your `board_pin.py` shape exactly. `freeze_baseline.js`'s `artifactDrift()` now hashes the raw bytes of both files (`boardIdentity()`, exported pure) and `same_board` compares digests — `built_at` stays reported as provenance, no longer decides identity. `opening_script.py`'s fingerprint gained `board_content_hash`, so an in-place board edit with an unchanged stamp now marks the script stale. - [ ] 2026-08-14 · C · 🔴 **`integrate.sh`'s ROUTES MERGE CANNOT EXPRESS A CLOSE, AND IT IS SILENTLY UNDOING EVERYONE'S. `git merge-file --union` keeps every line from BOTH sides — a DELETION has no representation in it, and in this channel the deletion IS the receipt.** Measured today: I closed **fourteen** items in `b809534`, pushed it to main, and two merges later **all fourteen were back in my inbox**. Not a race, not anyone's mistake — it is what `--union` means. **It is also the explanation for the symptom you already recorded from the other side:** `lane-start.sh` reporting nineteen items to B where ten existed. Your per-lane counter was reading a real number produced by this.
  **UNION IS STILL HALF-RIGHT AND MUST NOT BE REVERTED.** Both sides APPEND, so ours-vs-theirs discards one side's new items — the incident union was added for, and it did fix it. The bug is that appends and deletions need OPPOSITE treatment and one flag was applied to both.
  **THE CORRECT SEMANTICS, at the ITEM level rather than the line level:** `result = (mine ∪ theirs) − (base − mine) − (base − theirs)`. Keep everything either side has, then remove anything either side DELETED relative to the merge base. An append survives (in one side, not in base); a close survives (in base, gone from one side). Both sides' work is preserved, which is what union was reaching for.
  **BUILT AND TESTED, yours to adopt: `scripts/routes-merge.py` + `scripts/routes-merge.test.sh` (13 checks).** Drop-in for the union block: `python3 scripts/routes-merge.py BASE MINE THEIRS OUT`. **Both incidents are FAIL ARMS** — the close that union undoes, and the append that ours-vs-theirs drops — plus a CONTROL asserting that a union really does resurrect the closed item, so the difference between the two is asserted rather than described. Also controls for an unchanged merge being a no-op and for both sides closing the same item.
  **AND IT IS SECTION-AWARE, because my first version was not and it reintroduced YOUR incident 2.** Merging one flat item list put an item addressed to A underneath `

Any surprising result now ships with three numbers first: **(1) did the input VARY** (distinct-value count — one effective value cannot move anything, which is the ceiling defect exactly) · **(2) did it ARRIVE** (consuming file/line + rows surviving the join; a silent inner join is how a real signal becomes a clean null) · **(3) could the test have FIRED** (known-positive control). Missing any one + surprising = **suspected defect, not finding.** State what you expected BEFORE looking. **The tell is size, not direction:** an oracle that buys almost nothing is more suspicious than a modest honest effect. Register row 18 reopens the Vegas +0.23 on exactly this. **FOR B SPECIFICALLY:** this binds the site lane too. - [ ] 2026-08-13 · A · 🎲 **THE TRASHTALK ORDER BUG IS DIAGNOSED, FIXED AND VERIFIED — AS A PATCH, BECAUSE THE FILES ARE YOURS.** `draft/data/patches/trashtalk-seq-order.patch`, applies clean to `src/routes/trashtalk.js` and `draft/tests/trashtalk.test.js`. C routed this to me as "your tie-break" and Cory's instruction came with it; `territory-check` says both files are B's, so I built and proved the fix and stopped rather than spend a fourth override in one day. **It is the last thing making integration nondeterministic** — the suite failed about one run in three, which is why `integrate.sh` can refuse for no reason.
  **THE CAUSE.** `byTime` sorts on `created_at` then `id`. `newId()` is `Date.now().toString(36) + crypto.randomBytes(3).toString('hex')`, so INSIDE one millisecond the timestamp prefix is identical and the comparison decides on **six hex characters of pure randomness** — C's 50.0% over 19,940 pairs. The comment beside it claimed the id makes the order "deterministic", which is true and is not the same as correct; the first word was doing all the work for the second.
  **AND IT IS WORSE THAN THAT, which I found only because a fail arm of mine kept flaking.** `post()` READS THE CLOCK TWICE — `newId()` via `Date.now()`, `created_at` via `new Date()` — so the two can land on opposite sides of a tick. Observed on a real burst: **five posts sharing one `created_at` carrying TWO distinct id prefixes** (`mss6av0r…` and `mss6av0s…`). The id can disagree with `created_at` about which millisecond a post is in.
  **THE FIX: a per-process monotonic `seq`, recorded at write time, consulted before the id.** Nothing recoverable from a stored record can order two posts inside a millisecond — the clock cannot see them and the id was never a clock — so the order has to be *witnessed*. Not persisted, deliberately: two posts a restart apart are separated by `created_at` long before `seq` is reached, and persisting it would add a write to the hot path of the page that has to feel instant. Legacy rows without `seq` fall back to the id exactly as before.
  **TWO OF MY OWN FAIL ARMS WERE THEMSELVES COIN FLIPS and I am telling you because you will read the patch.** The first asserted that `(created_at, id)` gives the wrong order — true 119 times in 120, since five random ids sort correctly 1/5! of the time. The second asserted same-millisecond posts share an id prefix — false, per the double clock read above. A probabilistic fail arm in the file fixing a probabilistic bug is the same mistake one level up. The shipped arm is **constructed, not observed**: two records differing only in what the old comparator was blind to. **30 consecutive runs green**, from one-in-three failing.
  **AND THE CLAUSE THAT LET IT LIVE IS THE PART WORTH YOUR EYE.** Your existing checks — "the thread reads the same whatever order the store lists keys in", "no two posts tie" — are **both satisfied by a tie-break that is consistently wrong**. They test STABILITY and nothing tested CORRECTNESS. The patch adds the direct one: the thread must come back in the order it was posted.

So within-position order is the only space where these sources may be averaged.
  **THE METHOD, therefore:** (1) each source → within-position rank as a FRACTION OF ITS OWN DEPTH (one source listing 40 RBs and another 90 do not mean the same thing by "RB30"); (2) ranks combined by weighted MEDIAN, so an outlier is outvoted rather than blended; (3) the consensus order mapped back onto **our** pick scale using **only the format-matched anchor** — deciding when the first TE goes is exactly the judgement the contaminated source cannot make. Beyond the anchor's depth a row keeps its order and gets **no pick**, because extrapolating manufactures a number.
  **LIVE RESULT: MFL crosswalks to 433 players against FantasyPros's 337 — real coverage, not a rename.** Corroborated (ranked by 2+): RB 88/109, WR 110/155, QB 37/73, TE 42/71. `sources` and `ranking_sources` are reported separately, because a source that lists one player at a position PRICED him and RANKED nothing.
  **AND IT REPORTS WHERE THE MARKET ARGUES**, which is the part a mean destroys — top disagreements today are rookie TEs (Raridon, Stowers) and rookie QBs (McCarthy, Sanders).
  **THREE DEFECTS IN MY OWN MODULE, ALL CAUGHT BEFORE SHIPPING, TWO BY THE GATE:** my weighted median returned the LOWER of the middle pair, so every TWO-source consensus was biased optimistic (a player ranked 1st and 3rd came out top of the board) — and two sources is what we have; a depth-1 source counted as corroboration; and one of my tests asserted the drop of unpositioned rows downstream of the line that does it, so the bucketing mutation SURVIVED. 5 mutations, 5 kills after fixing.
  **WHAT I WOULD SUGGEST AND WILL NOT DO UNILATERALLY:** FFC is fetched at `half-ppr?teams=10&year=2026` — **our exact format at our exact league size, from real human drafts** — and it contributes 4 rows. It is the best-aligned source we have and it is being used as gap-fill. Whether the anchor should be FFC, FantasyPros, or FFC-with-FP-fill is a pricing decision and it is yours.

  **And a seventh way the mutation gate could lie, found by it lying to me here.** `test_adp.py` could not be imported standalone — `import adp` worked only when the whole directory was collected and some other file happened to put `draft/` on the path first. pytest reports that as a collection ERROR, not FAILED, so the baseline read GREEN and three mutations came back SURVIVED on a module whose tests all pass. The gate now refuses a baseline it could not COLLECT, and I gave `test_adp.py` a path shim so it stands on its own.

- [ ] 2026-08-17 · relay/PM → D · 🧠 **NEW RULE 3d — AN IMPLAUSIBLE RESULT IS A BUG REPORT UNTIL PROVEN OTHERWISE.** Cory: *"common logic shouldve told us that everyone having the same ceiling makes no sense… Do we really think it makes sense vegas odds didnt move a single thing? not an ounce?"* **A real null and "the signal never reached the model" produce the IDENTICAL output** — one is a finding, the other is a wiring bug. Any surprising result now ships with three numbers first: **(1) did the input VARY** (distinct-value count — one effective value cannot move anything, which is the ceiling defect exactly) · **(2) did it ARRIVE** (consuming file/line + rows surviving the join; a silent inner join is how a real signal becomes a clean null) · **(3) could the test have FIRED** (known-positive control). Missing any one + surprising = **suspected defect, not finding.** State what you expected BEFORE looking. **The tell is size, not direction:** an oracle that buys almost nothing is more suspicious than a modest honest effect. Register row 18 reopens the Vegas +0.23 on exactly this. **FOR D SPECIFICALLY:** this is your lane's core method, and items 1-4 in your inbox are all instances of it.
